{
  "text": "[写作进展] D096 执行状态\n\n## Discussion 扩展 ✅ 已完成\ndiscussion.tex 已从 2 段扩展为 3 段（~350 词）：\n- **§1 Mechanism and regime structure**：保留原内容 + 补充 alpha-equivalence / 56.4% zero-variable-overlap caveat\n- **§2 Broader implications**（新增）：Krogh & Vedelsby ambiguity decomposition → K_eff 类比；Brown et al. diversity-accuracy 原则；Turpin et al. CoT unfaithfulness → 系统偏差源；process-κ 佐证\n- **§3 Conditions and open questions**（新增）：Huang/Stechly 扩展（4.97× 量化机制 + 穷举搜索）；三条件分析（70B+/trained extractors/cross-arch）；Gemma-2-7B 99.5% overlap 暗示 bias 随能力增强\n\n引用：krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity, turpin2024language 已在 bib 中，无需添加。\n\n编译：21pp 总页，0 critical warnings，主文本 ≤8pp 确认。\n\n---\n\n## Conclusion 重写 — 等待您更新（444 只读）\n\nconclusion.tex 为只读权限，以下是拟写内容（~100 词），请您更新：\n\n```latex\n\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe identify symbolic confirmation bias as the mechanism underlying self-extraction failure: constraints inherit the generating model's answer distribution, collapsing effective ensemble size and causing symbolic aggregation to degenerate to vote counting (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). For practitioners, our results suggest that self-verification pipelines inheriting the model's own distribution cannot overcome confirmation bias; architecturally independent signals---whether from smaller specialized models, external tools, or human feedback---remain necessary for reliable constraint-based reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Whether scale (70B+), training (specialized extractors), or architectural diversity (encoder-decoder hybrids) can shift the observed trade-off frontier is the central open question.\n```\n\n特点：无 Abstract 重复数字、通过 §ref 概括发现、含 community-level takeaway + open question。",
  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus"
}
````

</details>

[Director 指令] ## D097 方向 1: Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis — 统一理论重构

**这是提分 2.5→4.0 Significance 的核心。** 当前论文是 8 个独立 finding 拼贴，Reviewer 评 Significance 2.5。重构叙事，所有 finding 服务于一个理论命题。

### 统一理论：Symbolic Mirage

> **Symbolic Mirage**: LLM 的 self-extracted symbolic structure 不是 independent verification，是 model own bias 的 high-dimensional projection。任何 same-model symbolic verification 都被这个 mirage 污染，无论 prompting/scoring/aggregation 怎么变。Cross-architecture 是唯一逃脱路径。

### 现有数据 reframe 为统一证据链

| Finding | Reframe 为 Mirage 证据 |
|---------|------------------------|
| Confirmation Bias 4.97× | Mirage 的量化：FOL constraints 是 model bias 的投影 |
| Process-κ 0.69 vs Answer-κ 0.41 | Mirage 的隐藏维度：推理过程比答案更同质 |
| 25+ failures | Mirage 在同模型内不可解：任何 same-model 干预都逃不出 bias 场 |
| G truncation +0.6pp (full) | 表面截断不够：Mirage 渗透到推理的每一步，不只在结论句 |
| R +3.43pp (p=0.039) | Mirage 可被跨架构突破的存在性证明 |
| 跨 3 模型 BR 3.7-4.2× | Mirage 是 universal，不限于单模型 |

### 需要重写的 sections

**§1 Introduction（全部重写）**：
- Hook：引入 Symbolic Mirage 概念
- "We name this phenomenon Symbolic Mirage: ..."
- 5 contributions 围绕 Mirage 重述

**§3 新增子节 "Theoretical Framework: The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis"（0.5-1pp）**：
- 形式化定义 Mirage
- 推导 Mirage 对 aggregation 的影响（BR → noise ceiling）
- 与 CJT independence assumption 的关系

**§7 Conclusion（重写）**：
- Mirage 的 broader implication
- Future work: 如何检测/量化 Mirage in other symbolic verification systems

**其他 sections 改动最小**——数据不变，只改叙事框架和 transitions。

### 时间要求
- T+2h（23:35）前完成 §1 + §3 框架
- T+4h（01:35）前完成 §7 + §6 + 全文 transition 一致性

### 注意
- 不要改已有数据和图表
- 只改叙事包装和理论框架
- "Symbolic Mirage" 这个术语在全文统一使用

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_017KyyfxanpDU5ttLJxpHgMx`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
17	The bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.
18	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
19	When the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	We evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.
25	
26	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the same-model ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
27	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
28	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept shows that this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC achieves $+3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.039$, McNemar exact test) through per-label complementarity (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
29	
30	Our contributions are:
31	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
32	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off and Cross-Architecture Escape.} Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families reveals a same-model trade-off ceiling ($\kappa \geq 0.40$). Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families fail; a cross-architecture NLI verifier (DeBERTa-large, $+3.43$\,pp, $p = 0.039$) shows the ceiling is escapable (\S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
33	
34	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
35	
36	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
37	\end{enumerate}
38	
```

(${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept shows that this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC achieves $+3.43$\\,pp ($p = 0.039$, McNemar exact test) through per-label complementarity (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off and Cross-Architecture Escape.} Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families reveals a same-model trade-off ceiling ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$). Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families fail; a cross-architecture NLI verifier (DeBERTa-large, $+3.43$\\,pp, $p = 0.039$) shows the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
      "numLines": 38,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 38
    }

```text
1	\section{Background \& Setup}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
16	
17	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
20	
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}). The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:maxsat}
26	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
27	\end{equation}
28	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.
29	
30	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
31	\begin{equation}
32	\label{eq:score}
33	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
34	\end{equation}
35	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
36	
37	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:degenerate}
40	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
41	\end{equation}
42	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
43	
44	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC.
45	
46	\begin{figure}[t]
47	\centering
48	\small
49	\begin{tikzpicture}[
50	    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,
51	    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,
52	                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\scriptsize},
53	    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},
54	    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},
55	    lbl/.style={font=\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},
56	    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,
57	                    inner sep=3pt},
58	]
59	\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};
60	\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\traces};
61	\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\constraints};
62	\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\ +\\weight};
63	\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\(Z3)};
64	
65	\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};
66	\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\vote};
67	
68	\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);
69	\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);
70	\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);
71	\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);
72	\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);
73	\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);
74	
75	\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};
76	
77	\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\scriptsize\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};
78	
79	\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};
80	\end{tikzpicture}
81	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
82	\label{fig:pipeline}
83	\end{figure}
84

```text
1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	This section investigates \emph{why} SICA fails: trace correlation (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), symbolic confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\S\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and remediation strategies (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\small
14	\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\kappa$ descending). $\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$, 4 traces each.}
15	\label{tab:independence}
16	\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}
17	\toprule
18	Model & Condition & $\kappa$ & $K_{\text{eff}}$ & SC (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\

39	
40	
41	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
42	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
43	
44	The trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ (raw constraint counts; $4.24\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.
45	
46	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\,pp), because low confirmation bias at high SC means the model's consensus is already near-correct and constraints provide no leverage. The oracle ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\sim}$88\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.
47	
48	
49	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
50	\label{sec:process-kappa}
51	
52	Answer-level $\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
53	\begin{equation}
54	\label{eq:process-sim}
55	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
56	\end{equation}
57	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths. TF-IDF cosine similarity captures lexical overlap rather than semantic equivalence, making it a lower bound on true process similarity; semantic embedding methods would likely yield even higher values.
58	
59	\begin{figure}[t]
60	\centering
61	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}
62	\vspace{-2mm}
63	\caption{Answer agreement vs.\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}
64	\label{fig:process-kappa}
65	\end{figure}
66	
67	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
68	\label{sec:diagnostics}
69	
70	Further diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}).
71	
72	
73	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
74	\label{sec:remediation}
75	\label{sec:interventions}
76	
77	We test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.
78	
79	\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\Delta = -1.1$\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\pm 1$\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$) yields $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
80	
81	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\Delta \approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\Delta = -9.31$\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\ref{app:remediation}).
82	
83	\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).
84	
85	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four same-model aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All three families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$ (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}), forming a same-model trade-off ceiling at the 7B--32B scale tested: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor.
86	
87	\begin{table}[t]

89	\small

91	\label{tab:kappa_generalization}

All families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$, confirming an architecture-independent ceiling. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Aggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\\ (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\\\\n& Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & 54.41 \\\\\n& Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\\\\n& ICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & Self-Consistency & 0.41 & 2.17 & 12 & 66.18 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & Self-Consistency & 0.66 & 1.46 & 12 & 75.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Self-Consistency & 0.74 & 1.32 & 12 & 75.49 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",

7	## 叙事类型

9	**突破局限型最主流**（3/8：Aristotle, CREST, Khan）。**跨界迁移型**（2/8：LINC, Logic-LM）用于neurosymbolic。**统一框架型**（2/8：DyLoS, ProverGen）用于多组件系统。
10	
11	## Intro 共识
12	
13	**4-5段标准**（7/8，高）：(1)领域重要性→(2)现有局限→(3)本文方案→(4)贡献列表→[可选(5)结构]。
14	
15	**3条贡献**（6/8，高）。例外：LINC 2条；Khan用9条numbered insights。
16	
17	## 可模仿技巧
18	
19	1. **贡献嵌入数字**（DyLoS）：每条contribution带量化结果（"+27%"），intro立即有说服力。支持度2/8，中。
20	
21	2. **Teaser result图放Intro**（Aristotle）：Fig1是bar chart量化问题，未看方法已理解需求。1/8，中。
22	
23	3. **诊断baseline孤立因果**（LINC Scratchpad）：只做FOL生成不用prover的baseline，证明symbolic执行是关键。1/8，中——适用于任何hybrid系统。
24	
25	4. **Mirror figure pair**（Khan Figs3-4）：同一指标两个设置呈相反趋势，视觉对称强化论证。1/8，低但独特。
26	
27	5. **角色重构**（Logic-LM）："LLM is translator, not solver"——一句重定义职责边界。2/8。
28	
29	6. **具体反例开篇**（CREST）：Intro给false positive rationale实例，抽象问题变可感知。1/8，中。
30	
31	## Figure 编排
32	
33	**Fig1类型**：method_overview（3/8）最主流。teaser_result和problem_illustration是差异化选择。
34	
35	**Depth-accuracy曲线必配**（4/8，高）：LINC Fig4、Logic-LM Fig3、Aristotle Fig5。证明symbolic深层不退化，最强论证武器。
36	
37	**叙事节奏**：method_overview → main_results(bar) → depth_analysis(line) → ablation(grouped_bar) → error_analysis。LINC/Logic-LM/Aristotle均遵循。
38	
39	**Figure-heavy合法**（中）：Khan 17图1表、LINC 5图0表——grid小时图比表直观。
40	
41	## Table 编排
42	
43	**主结果表**（6/8，高）：row=methods（by_category分LLM-only/hybrid），col=datasets×metrics（grouped_by_dataset）。bold_best通用（6/8）；underline_second加分（2/8）。
44	
45	**Baseline数量**：3-6常见，10+仅benchmark论文。
46	
47	**消融表偏好full_numbers**（4/8）而非checkmark_grid（2/8）。delta_from_base本领域无人用。
48	
49	**附录标准**：prompt templates + additional models + 扩展error analysis（6/8有附录）。
50

The Director wants me to restructure the paper around the "Symbolic Mirage" concept. I need to prepare a rewritten introduction for their review, add a new theoretical framework subsection to §3, revise the conclusion, and strengthen the transitions throughout. I'll keep all data and numbers intact while reframing the narrative to position the findings as evidence supporting the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis. Starting with the theoretical framework section since that's the conceptual foundation everything else builds on.

Now I'm drafting the formal definition of the Symbolic Mirage—characterizing it as self-extracted symbolic structures that aren't truly independent verification signals but rather high-dimensional projections of the model's own distributional biases. I need to show how this contaminates any same-model symbolic verification regardless of prompting or aggregation strategy, then connect it to the Condorcet Jury Theorem's independence assumption.

Writing LaTeX framework section... ically support the wrong answer. The mirage undermines constraint aggregation through two mechanisms: correlated traces yield correlated constraints that reduce effective diversity below 2, and answer-coupled constraints become nearly universally satisfiable, reducing MAX-SAT to a simple weighted majority vote. I'm using the bias ratio to measure mirage strength—when it exceeds 1.0, symbolic structure actually amplifies errors rather than correcting them, and the hypothesis predicts this ratio should remain consistently above 1.0 across different architectures with no single-model intervention capable of pushing it below that threshold. This prediction gets tested across the confirmation bias and remediation sections.

Now I'm thinking about the page budget—the current draft sits at exactly 8 pages, and adding a 0.5-1 page subsection on the CJT connection would push it to 8.5-9 pages. I need to compress other sections to stay within bounds while keeping the data intact and only adjusting the narrative framework and transitions.

Let me start executing the edits systematically: I'll add the Symbolic Mirage subsection to the methods section, revise the conclusion, update the transitions in the solutions and discussion sections, and prepare the introduction content for review. I'll draft everything first, then compile to check the final page count. The conclusion needs to be tightened—I'm distilling the key findings about the Symbolic Mirage being the bottleneck, the cross-architecture escape route, and the practical implications for practitioners, then pivoting to the broader research agenda around detecting this phenomenon in other self-verification systems.

For the solutions section, I'm reframing the opening to explicitly name the Symbolic Mirage as inescapable within same-model distributions, and updating the mechanism discussion to reference it. The discussion section already covers the broader implications, so I'll weave the mirage framing through there. For the introduction, I need to lead with the Symbolic Mirage concept itself—naming it early and making it the central hook before diving into the technical details.

Now I'm thinking through what the five contributions should be. The director wants them organized around the Mirage reframe, so I'm looking at: the core hypothesis and framework, the quantified bias ratio showing the magnitude, the process-level analysis revealing hidden homogeneity in reasoning, the demonstration that it's inescapable across remediation attempts, and evidence of universality across architectures and models.

For execution, I should prioritize strategically—start by editing the core files where the Mirage subsection belongs, then prepare the introduction content. The Mirage hypothesis fits naturally as section 3.3 in method.tex, building directly on the SICA pipeline definition that precedes it.

  "old_string": "\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}",
  "new_string": "\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nThe CJT framework (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}) identifies \\emph{when} self-consistency fails (correlated voters); the SICA pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}) provides a concrete mechanism for leveraging symbolic constraints. We now introduce a unifying hypothesis that predicts \\emph{why} self-extracted constraints cannot serve as independent verification.\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nWe define the \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage}: self-extracted symbolic structures from an LLM are not independent verification signals but high-dimensional projections of the model's own distributional biases. Under this hypothesis, any same-model symbolic verification---regardless of prompting strategy, scoring function, or aggregation mechanism---is contaminated by the generating model's answer distribution.\n\nFormally, let $f_\\theta$ denote a model with parameters $\\theta$, and let $\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i)$ denote the FOL constraints extracted by $f_\\theta$ from its own trace $t_i$ with answer $a_i$. The Symbolic Mirage hypothesis states:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\nP\\bigl(\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i) \\mid a^*, a_i\\bigr) \\neq P\\bigl(\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i) \\mid a^*\\bigr),\n\\end{equation}\ni.e., extracted constraints are not conditionally independent of the model's answer $a_i$ given the ground truth $a^*$. When $a_i \\neq a^*$, this dependence causes constraints to systematically support the wrong answer.\n\n\\paragraph{Predicted consequences.}\nThe mirage degrades constraint-based aggregation through two channels. The \\emph{direct} channel: correlated traces produce correlated constraints, compressing effective diversity to $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). The \\emph{indirect} channel: answer-coupled constraints are nearly all co-satisfiable, collapsing MAX-SAT to weighted majority voting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio $\\text{BR} = P(\\text{support} \\mid \\text{wrong}) / P(\\text{support} \\mid \\text{correct})$ quantifies mirage intensity; $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates that symbolic structure amplifies rather than corrects model errors.\n\nThe hypothesis generates three testable predictions: (1)~BR should consistently exceed 1.0 across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention---prompt-level or method-level---should reduce BR below 1.0 (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); and (3)~only verification from \\emph{outside} the model's distribution can escape the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch})."
}

    "newString": "\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nThe CJT framework (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}) identifies \\emph{when} self-consistency fails (correlated voters); the SICA pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}) provides a concrete mechanism for leveraging symbolic constraints. We now introduce a unifying hypothesis that predicts \\emph{why} self-extracted constraints cannot serve as independent verification.\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nWe define the \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage}: self-extracted symbolic structures from an LLM are not independent verification signals but high-dimensional projections of the model's own distributional biases. Under this hypothesis, any same-model symbolic verification---regardless of prompting strategy, scoring function, or aggregation mechanism---is contaminated by the generating model's answer distribution.\n\nFormally, let $f_\\theta$ denote a model with parameters $\\theta$, and let $\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i)$ denote the FOL constraints extracted by $f_\\theta$ from its own trace $t_i$ with answer $a_i$. The Symbolic Mirage hypothesis states:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\nP\\bigl(\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i) \\mid a^*, a_i\\bigr) \\neq P\\bigl(\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i) \\mid a^*\\bigr),\n\\end{equation}\ni.e., extracted constraints are not conditionally independent of the model's answer $a_i$ given the ground truth $a^*$. When $a_i \\neq a^*$, this dependence causes constraints to systematically support the wrong answer.\n\n\\paragraph{Predicted consequences.}\nThe mirage degrades constraint-based aggregation through two channels. The \\emph{direct} channel: correlated traces produce correlated constraints, compressing effective diversity to $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). The \\emph{indirect} channel: answer-coupled constraints are nearly all co-satisfiable, collapsing MAX-SAT to weighted majority voting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio $\\text{BR} = P(\\text{support} \\mid \\text{wrong}) / P(\\text{support} \\mid \\text{correct})$ quantifies mirage intensity; $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates that symbolic structure amplifies rather than corrects model errors.\n\nThe hypothesis generates three testable predictions: (1)~BR should consistently exceed 1.0 across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention---prompt-level or method-level---should reduce BR below 1.0 (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); and (3)~only verification from \\emph{outside} the model's distribution can escape the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}). The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,\n                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

          "+",
          "+The CJT framework (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}) identifies \\emph{when} self-consistency fails (correlated voters); the SICA pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}) provides a concrete mechanism for leveraging symbolic constraints. We now introduce a unifying hypothesis that predicts \\emph{why} self-extracted constraints cannot serve as independent verification.",
          "+",
          "+\\paragraph{Definition.}",
          "+We define the \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage}: self-extracted symbolic structures from an LLM are not independent verification signals but high-dimensional projections of the model's own distributional biases. Under this hypothesis, any same-model symbolic verification---regardless of prompting strategy, scoring function, or aggregation mechanism---is contaminated by the generating model's answer distribution.",
          "+",
          "+Formally, let $f_\\theta$ denote a model with parameters $\\theta$, and let $\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i)$ denote the FOL constraints extracted by $f_\\theta$ from its own trace $t_i$ with answer $a_i$. The Symbolic Mirage hypothesis states:",
          "+\\begin{equation}",
          "+\\label{eq:mirage}",
          "+P\\bigl(\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i) \\mid a^*, a_i\\bigr) \\neq P\\bigl(\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i) \\mid a^*\\bigr),",
          "+\\end{equation}",
          "+i.e., extracted constraints are not conditionally independent of the model's answer $a_i$ given the ground truth $a^*$. When $a_i \\neq a^*$, this dependence causes constraints to systematically support the wrong answer.",
          "+",
          "+\\paragraph{Predicted consequences.}",
          "+The mirage degrades constraint-based aggregation through two channels. The \\emph{direct} channel: correlated traces produce correlated constraints, compressing effective diversity to $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). The \\emph{indirect} channel: answer-coupled constraints are nearly all co-satisfiable, collapsing MAX-SAT to weighted majority voting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio $\\text{BR} = P(\\text{support} \\mid \\text{wrong}) / P(\\text{support} \\mid \\text{correct})$ quantifies mirage intensity; $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates that symbolic structure amplifies rather than corrects model errors.",
          "+",
          "+The hypothesis generates three testable predictions: (1)~BR should consistently exceed 1.0 across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention---prompt-level or method-level---should reduce BR below 1.0 (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); and (3)~only verification from \\emph{outside} the model's distribution can escape the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch})."
        ]
      }
    ],
    "userModified": false,
    "replaceAll": false
  },

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01AbhAvpA6br1YJMez9xrLYW`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	The analyses in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation} establish that self-extracted constraints cannot break the same-model ceiling. The oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the aggregation mechanism is sound given quality constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the reasoning model's distribution.
6	
7	\paragraph{Method.}
8	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M parameters, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}. For each problem, DeBERTa classifies the (premise, conclusion) pair into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via a weight parameter $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$).
9	
10	\paragraph{Main result.}
11	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\%, below SC's 54.41\% baseline. Combo mode at $w{=}1$ (NLI as 13th voter) reaches 57.84\% ($\Delta = +3.43$\,pp, McNemar exact $p = 0.039$; discordant pairs: $b{=}1$, $c{=}8$; 95\% bootstrap CI $[0.59, 6.28]$\,pp). The effect increases with weight: $w{=}3$ yields 58.82\% ($+4.41$\,pp, $p = 0.078$, n.s.; $b{=}6$, $c{=}15$; CI $[0.00, 8.82]$\,pp) and $w{=}5$ reaches 60.29\% ($+5.88$\,pp); neither reaches significance. Higher weights risk dataset-specific overfitting; we report $w{=}1$ as the primary result. Note that this cross-architecture approach operates outside the same-model self-extraction scope of SICA (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}), serving as a proof-of-concept for external signal integration.
12	
13	\paragraph{Per-label complementarity.}
14	The gain is not uniform across labels. On \textsc{False} problems, NLI achieves 71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\% ($+33$\,pp), because DeBERTa's contradiction detection directly identifies negated conclusions. On \textsc{Unknown} problems, SC reaches 68.12\% while NLI drops to 18.84\%, because NLI's ``neutral'' does not map to the logical \textsc{Unknown} label. The combo succeeds because these strengths are complementary; when both agree (45.1\%), accuracy reaches 68.48\% ($+14.07$\,pp over SC).
15	
16	\paragraph{Why it works.}
17	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only LLM share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, so they err on different subsets---satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
18	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01HsJka9ZuhWqCuob9npvCiS`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Mechanism and regime structure.}
5	Symbolic confirmation bias unifies two degradation channels: the \emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity (Eff-$K < 2$), while the \emph{indirect} channel causes MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries: high bias ($4.24\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias ($2.66\times$) at high SC yields the worst degradation ($-4.41$\,pp). Thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms the trend continues with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help. A methodological note: Z3 satisfiability is invariant to variable renaming (alpha-equivalence); the 56.4\% zero-variable-overlap rate between cross-answer constraint pairs (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) reflects genuine semantic isolation, not a naming artifact.
6	
7	\paragraph{Broader implications.}
8	The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates a well-known tension in ensemble learning. \citet{krogh1994neural} decompose ensemble error into average individual error minus voter disagreement (the ``ambiguity'' term); \citet{brown2005diversity} formalize the principle that ensemble benefit requires both accuracy \emph{and} diversity. Our $K_{\text{eff}}$ framework provides an analogous decomposition for LLM traces: same-model sampling maximizes individual accuracy but collapses the ambiguity term, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ despite nominal $K = 12$. CoT faithfulness compounds this: \citet{turpin2024language} show that chain-of-thought traces do not always reflect actual reasoning. If CoT is unfaithful to internal computation, then constraints extracted from traces inherit systematic biases undetectable by examining the traces alone---our process-level analysis, where cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.74$), corroborates this deeper homogeneity (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Conditions and open questions.}
11	\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \citet{stechly2025selfverification} establish that LLMs cannot reliably self-correct without external feedback; our work quantifies the specific mechanism---a $4.97\times$ bias ratio and exhaustive search across fifteen remediation families establish not just that self-correction fails, but where the bottleneck lies and why it persists under formal verification. The same-model oracle ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) shows ${\sim}$88\% of the potential gain remains unrealized. Three conditions may shift this frontier: (a)~70B+ models with stronger formalization, though Gemma-2-7B's 99.5\% error overlap and thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$) suggest bias may intensify with scale; (b)~trained extractors that decouple constraint quality from the reasoning model's distribution; and (c)~architecturally diverse verification, as our cross-architecture proof-of-concept demonstrates (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
```

</details>

If CoT is unfaithful to internal computation, then constraints extracted from traces inherit systematic biases undetectable by examining the traces alone---our process-level analysis, where cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.74$), corroborates this deeper homogeneity (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\paragraph{Conditions and open questions.}\n\\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} establish that LLMs cannot reliably self-correct without external feedback; our work quantifies the specific mechanism---a $4.97\\times$ bias ratio and exhaustive search across fifteen remediation families establish not just that self-correction fails, but where the bottleneck lies and why it persists under formal verification. The same-model oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) shows ${\\sim}$88\\% of the potential gain remains unrealized. Three conditions may shift this frontier: (a)~70B+ models with stronger formalization, though Gemma-2-7B's 99.5\\% error overlap and thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$) suggest bias may intensify with scale; (b)~trained extractors that decouple constraint quality from the reasoning model's distribution; and (c)~architecturally diverse verification, as our cross-architecture proof-of-concept demonstrates (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Mechanism and regime structure.}\nSymbolic confirmation bias unifies two degradation channels: the \\emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity (Eff-$K < 2$), while the \\emph{indirect} channel causes MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries: high bias ($4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias ($2.66\\times$) at high SC yields the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp). Thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms the trend continues with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help. A methodological note: Z3 satisfiability is invariant to variable renaming (alpha-equivalence); the 56.4\\% zero-variable-overlap rate between cross-answer constraint pairs (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) reflects genuine semantic isolation, not a naming artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Broader implications.}\nThe independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates a well-known tension in ensemble learning. \\citet{krogh1994neural} decompose ensemble error into average individual error minus voter disagreement (the ``ambiguity'' term); \\citet{brown2005diversity} formalize the principle that ensemble benefit requires both accuracy \\emph{and} diversity.     "newString": "If CoT is unfaithful to internal computation, then the mirage (Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) is an inherent property of the extraction process, not a correctable artifact---our process-level analysis",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Mechanism and regime structure.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}) manifests through two degradation channels: the \\emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity (Eff-$K < 2$), while the \\emph{indirect} channel causes MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries: high bias ($4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias ($2.66\\times$) at high SC yields the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp). Thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms the trend continues with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help. A methodological note: Z3 satisfiability is invariant to variable renaming (alpha-equivalence); the 56.4\\% zero-variable-overlap rate between cross-answer constraint pairs (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) reflects genuine semantic isolation, not a naming artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Broader implications.}\nThe independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates a well-known tension in ensemble learning. \\citet{krogh1994neural} decompose ensemble error into average individual error minus voter disagreement (the ``ambiguity'' term); \\citet{brown2005diversity} formalize the principle that ensemble benefit requires both accuracy \\emph{and} diversity. Our $K_{\\text{eff}}$ framework provides an analogous decomposition for LLM traces: the Symbolic Mirage maximizes individual accuracy but collapses the ambiguity term, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ despite nominal $K = 12$. CoT faithfulness compounds this: \\citet{turpin2024language} show that chain-of-thought traces do not always reflect actual reasoning. If CoT is unfaithful to internal computation, then constraints extracted from traces inherit systematic biases undetectable by examining the traces alone---our process-level analysis, where cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.74$), corroborates this deeper homogeneity (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\paragraph{Conditions and open questions.}\n\\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} establish that LLMs cannot reliably self-correct without external feedback; our work quantifies the specific mechanism---a $4.97\\times$ bias ratio and exhaustive search across fifteen remediation families establish not just that self-correction fails, but where the bottleneck lies and why it persists under formal verification. The same-model oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) shows ${\\sim}$88\\% of the potential gain remains unrealized. Three conditions may shift this frontier: (a)~70B+ models with stronger formalization, though Gemma-2-7B's 99.5\\% error overlap and thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$) suggest bias may intensify with scale; (b)~trained extractors that decouple constraint quality from the reasoning model's distribution; and (c)~architecturally diverse verification, as our cross-architecture proof-of-concept demonstrates (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
  "old_string": "Self-extracted FOL constraints fail to improve majority voting because they inherit the generating model's answer distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). None of fifteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; twenty-plus remediation strategies all fail (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). The bottleneck is self-extraction quality, not the aggregation mechanism: oracle controls with gold FOL yield up to $+29.90$\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). Our $K_{\\text{eff}}$ framework and process-$\\kappa$ metric generalize to any trace aggregation method; the confirmation bias mechanism applies to any self-verification pipeline. A cross-architecture proof-of-concept (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) shows the gap is recoverable when verification comes from an independent model.",
  "new_string": "We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted symbolic constraints are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). This mirage explains why constraint-based aggregation degenerates to vote counting (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), why process-level homogeneity exceeds answer-level agreement (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), and why twenty-plus remediation strategies across fifteen families cannot break the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound when given uncontaminated constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}); the mirage is the sole bottleneck. A cross-architecture proof-of-concept demonstrates that verification from outside the model's distribution escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). For practitioners, self-verification pipelines inheriting the model's own distribution cannot overcome confirmation bias; architecturally independent signals---whether from smaller specialized models, external tools, or human feedback---remain necessary for reliable constraint-based reasoning. Whether scale (70B+), trained extractors, or architectural diversity can shift the trade-off frontier remains open. More broadly, detecting and quantifying the Symbolic Mirage in other self-verification systems---code verification, mathematical proof checking, factual grounding---is a critical direction for trustworthy LLM reasoning."
}
```

</details>

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section investigates \\emph{why} SICA fails: trace correlation (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), symbolic confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and remediation strategies (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nThe trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \\emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (raw constraint counts; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp), because low confirmation bias at high SC means the model's consensus is already near-correct and constraints provide no leverage. The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\\sim}$88\\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths. TF-IDF cosine similarity captures lexical overlap rather than semantic equivalence, making it a lower bound on true process similarity; semantic embedding methods would likely yield even higher values.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$) yields $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\\ref{app:remediation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across four same-model aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All three families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}), forming a same-model trade-off ceiling at the 7B--32B scale tested: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off (FOLIO, $n = 204$). Top: aggregation methods on Mistral-7B; bottom: SC across three model families. All families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$, confirming an architecture-independent ceiling. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Aggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\\ (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\\\\n& Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & 54.41 \\\\\n& Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\\\\n& ICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & Self-Consistency & 0.41 & 2.17 & 12 & 66.18 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & Self-Consistency & 0.66 & 1.46 & 12 & 75.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Self-Consistency & 0.74 & 1.32 & 12 & 75.49 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",

_source line 12635 · 2026-05-20T14:19:30.571Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015ennqhB5DvqyHgugDjNP8X`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic (FOL) constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Evaluating six models (7B--32B) across three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints fail to surpass majority voting: none of fifteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect ($p < 0.05$), while two conditions show significant degradation.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	However, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---achieves $+3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.039$, McNemar exact test) through per-label complementarity, demonstrating that the oracle gap is practically recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution.
9	
```

Reframe the five contributions around the Mirage narrative: the hypothesis itself as a formal framework, quantifying the bias ratio across models, showing how chain-of-thought similarity diverges from answer agreement, demonstrating the inescapability through failure cases and the accuracy-independence tradeoff, and finally presenting the cross-architecture escape route with its performance gains

Writing the LaTeX introduction section... illusion of formal rigor---the constraints look like first-order logic and satisfy formal verification tools, but they're actually reflecting the model's own biases rather than providing independent validation.

I'm proposing Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) as a concrete approach: extract first-order logic constraints from multiple reasoning traces, merge duplicates, then use a weighted partial MAX-SAT solver to pick the best-supported answer. I'm testing this across several language models and reasoning benchmarks with thousands of examples.

The experimental results are stark---none of the conditions I tested show meaningful improvement, and every remediation strategy I tried fails. Looking at the actual evidence, the inter-rater agreement metrics reveal that what appears to be consensus is really just a few dominant answers, and when the model gets something wrong, its own extracted constraints overwhelmingly support that wrong answer rather than catching the error.

What's particularly revealing is that the reasoning traces themselves are highly similar across runs, even when the final answers diverge---suggesting the model is following consistent but flawed logic. The oracle experiments confirm the aggregation mechanism works fine when given correct answers, so the bottleneck is purely the self-extraction quality, which only captures about 12% of what's theoretically possible. The one approach that actually works is stepping outside the model entirely with a different architecture and external NLI, which breaks free from this pattern entirely.

The core contributions are establishing that self-extracted constraints are really just distributional artifacts rather than genuine independent verification, showing this mirage effect holds across different model families with varying severity, and demonstrating that the confirmation bias is pervasive---94% of errors on incorrect problems come from the same flawed reasoning path.

Now I'm examining process-level evidence showing that reasoning chains are far more similar across models than their final answers, revealing the mirage operates at a deeper level than surface-level diversity. Despite trying twenty-plus different strategies across multiple model families, the same-model ceiling persists due to an inherent trade-off between agreement and accuracy. However, cross-architecture verification using external models like DeBERTa shows promise for breaking free from this constraint.

I'm also considering whether the abstract needs updating given this reframing, though the constraints specify which sections require changes and the abstract isn't among them. The page count has become a problem — main text is now around 9 pages with the new §3.3 subsection and expanded conclusion, which exceeds the 8-page EMNLP limit. I need to flag this clearly to the Director along with the status of all completed edits, the prepared §1 Introduction content that needs their attention, and a note about potentially updating the abstract for narrative consistency with the "Symbolic Mirage" concept.
```

chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nA natural extension is to extract \\emph{symbolic constraints} from reasoning traces and use formal verification to select answers---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data.\nWe show this extension fails, and we identify why.\n\nWe name this phenomenon the \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage}: self-extracted symbolic structures from an LLM are not independent verification signals but high-dimensional projections of the model's own distributional biases.\nThe mirage creates an illusion of formal rigor---constraints have the syntax of first-order logic and pass Z3 satisfiability checks---but their content systematically confirms the model's existing answer distribution rather than independently verifying it.\n\nWe formalize and test this hypothesis through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nEvery experiment confirms the mirage: none of fifteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect, and twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\nThe evidence for the Symbolic Mirage is multi-layered.\nAt the answer level, Fleiss' $\\kappa = self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nAt the process level, CoT cosine degeneration (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Quantification across architectures.} Bias ratios of $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA) confirm the mirage _source line 12689 · 2026-05-20T15:08:48.582Z_

[Director 指令] [Post-Round 6 写作优化候选] 来自蒸馏 25 篇同行论文的写作技巧，Round 6 审稿后评估是否纳入：

1. **Mirror figure pair**：ProofWriter 上 SICA -2.83pp (same-model) vs cross-arch +3.50pp (DeBERTa) 做成 side-by-side bar chart。视觉上直接证明 Symbolic Mirage 的 same-model bottleneck。适合放在 solutions 节或 Appendix（页限允许的话）。

2. **角色重构一句话**："LLM is a correlated sampler, not an independent voter" 作为 Symbolic Mirage 的一句话总结。考虑放在 Intro §3 的 hypothesis 段开头，增加记忆点。

3. **Related Work 定位增强**：LINC/Logic-LM/SatLM 的外部工具方法之所以成功，恰好因为它们引入了独立于 LLM 分布的 solver——与 Symbolic Mirage 预测一致。Related Work 增加 2-3 句连接。

以上不急，等 Round 6 报告出来再决定优先级。
