## 1. 标题修改 [W1]
原：Beyond Self-Consistency: Why Self-Extracted Symbolic Constraints Fail in LLM Reasoning
改：Beyond Self-Consistency: Why Self-Extracted Symbolic Constraints Do Not Improve LLM Reasoning

## 2. Symbolic Mirage Scoping [W10]
在 method.tex §3.3 Symbolic Mirage 首次定义处，紧跟 "Symbolic Mirage" 粗体后加限定句：
"The mirage is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (§\ref{sec:oracle})."

## 3. Novelty Distinction [W3]
在 Discussion (§7) 第三段（Conditions and open questions）前插入新段落，明确区分：
- Huang et al. (2024): qualitative "LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback"
- Stechly et al. (2025): qualitative "self-verification is unreliable"
- 本文增量：(a) K_eff + BR 作为可复用诊断工具（不是仅定性结论），(b) formal verification (Z3) 也无法突破——超越 prompt-level self-correction，(c) process-level similarity 揭示比 answer-level 更深的同质性

约 5-6 句，需要裁剪等量内容。

## 4. Post-hoc Taxonomy [W7]
在 experiments.tex Table 1 caption 或正文中，Type A/B/C 分类处加 "empirical" 修饰：
"We identify three empirical regimes (thresholds observation-driven, not theoretically derived)"

## 5. Bias Ratio Clarification [W6]
§5.2 (analysis.tex) 中 "4.97×" 首次出现处加注：
"(pooled raw ratio for Mistral-7B FOLIO default seed; per-model ratios in Table 14)"

## 6. Reasoning Models in §2 [W14]
Related Work 加 1-2 句：reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1, QwQ) 延长 thinking time 但仍受 independence bottleneck——QwQ-32B κ=0.91 说明 thinking mode 加剧 homogenization。

在 experiments.tex Oracle Control 段或 appendix 加 1 句：
"ProofWriter provides programmatic depth-stratified rules from which DSL constraints are automatically derived, requiring no human annotation."

## 8. PoC Dataset Justification [Q6]
在 solutions.tex Method 段末加 1 句：
"We evaluate on FOLIO and ProofWriter because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs, which these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) and mathematical reasoning (GSM8K) lack this structure."

- Discussion §7 段 1 "Mechanism and regime structure"：process-κ per-problem AUROC 已证 negative，相关讨论可压缩

- Figure 1 (pipeline) 尽量提前到 page 2-3

83	\end{figure}

85	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
86	\label{sec:mirage}
87	
88	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. Formally, let $\mathcal{E}_\theta(t_i)$ denote constraints extracted by model $f_\theta$ from trace $t_i$ with answer $a_i$:
89	\begin{equation}
90	\label{eq:mirage}
91	P\bigl(\mathcal{E}_\theta(t_i) \mid a^*, a_i\bigr) \neq P\bigl(\mathcal{E}_\theta(t_i) \mid a^*\bigr),
92	\end{equation}
93	i.e., extracted constraints depend on the model's answer $a_i$ given truth $a^*$. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio $\text{BR} = P(\text{support} \mid \text{wrong}) / P(\text{support} \mid \text{correct})$ quantifies mirage intensity. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below 1.0 (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
94

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Mechanism and regime structure.}
5	The Symbolic Mirage (\S\ref{sec:mirage}) manifests through two degradation channels: the \emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity (Eff-$K < 2$), while the \emph{indirect} channel causes MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries: high bias ($4.24\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias ($2.66\times$) at high SC yields the worst degradation ($-4.41$\,pp). Thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms the trend continues with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help. A methodological note: Z3 satisfiability is invariant to variable renaming (alpha-equivalence); the 56.4\% zero-variable-overlap rate between cross-answer constraint pairs (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) reflects genuine semantic isolation, not a naming artifact.
6	
7	\paragraph{Broader implications.}
8	The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates a well-known tension in ensemble learning. \citet{krogh1994neural} decompose ensemble error into average individual error minus voter disagreement (the ``ambiguity'' term); \citet{brown2005diversity} formalize the principle that ensemble benefit requires both accuracy \emph{and} diversity. Our $K_{\text{eff}}$ framework provides an analogous decomposition for LLM traces: the Symbolic Mirage maximizes individual accuracy but collapses the ambiguity term, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ despite nominal $K = 12$. CoT faithfulness compounds this: \citet{turpin2024language} show that chain-of-thought traces do not always reflect actual reasoning. If CoT is unfaithful to internal computation, then the mirage (Eq.~\ref{eq:mirage}) is an inherent property of the extraction process, not a correctable artifact---our process-level analysis, where cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.74$), corroborates this deeper homogeneity (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Conditions and open questions.}
11	The Symbolic Mirage provides a mechanistic explanation for why LLMs cannot reliably self-correct \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}: a $4.97\times$ bias ratio persists across fifteen remediation families. Three conditions may shift the frontier: (a)~70B+ models, though thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$) suggests bias may intensify with scale; (b)~trained extractors decoupled from the reasoning model's distribution; and (c)~architecturally diverse verification (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations across ${\sim}$1,250 unique instances. Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\S\ref{sec:analysis}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold FOL annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$; extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$. Z3 timeout: 10s. $\alpha{=}0.5$. Statistical significance via McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs from 10,000 resamples for $n \geq 200$.
14	
15	\paragraph{Computational cost.} On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($K{=}12$), SICA takes $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock time; constraint extraction ($K$ additional LLM calls) accounts for 76\% of overhead, Z3 solving is negligible (${<}0.1\%$).
16	
17	
18	\subsection{Main Results}
19	\label{sec:main-results}
20	
21	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. SICA effectiveness depends sharply on the accuracy regime.
22	
23	\begin{table*}[t]

64	
65	\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\sim$50--55\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds: $\Delta = +3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.092$) and $+2.94$\,pp ($p = 0.070$); the remaining seeds show null or negative results. No seed reaches $p < 0.05$; the 4-seed mean is $+1.47$\,pp. The default-seed and seed-456 runs share identical SC baselines (55.88\%) yet diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\ $0.00$\,pp), suggesting that seed-level constraint quality, not SC level alone, influences the effect (entropy decomposition in Appendix~\ref{app:entropy}). Mistral-7B on LogiQA, despite the same SC range, yields only $\Delta = +0.50$\,pp ($p = 1.0$), attributable to domain formalizability (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}).
66	
67	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%): confirmation bias.} All nine Type~A conditions (SC $\geq 60\%$, combined $N > 2{,}800$) produce no significant positive effects. The largest positive trend is LLaMA-3.1-8B on StrategyQA ($\Delta = +2.00$\,pp, $p = 0.424$, n.s.), while Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$). Even in-context learning with gold FOL examples ($\Delta = +0.50$\,pp, n.s.) cannot break confirmation bias.
68	
69	\paragraph{Type C (SC $\leq$ 40\%): error amplification.} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\%$) shows $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning.
70	
71	\paragraph{Degenerate cases.} When $C_0 < 5\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.
72	
73	The regime scatter (Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter}, Appendix) confirms this pattern visually.
74	
75	
76	\subsection{Oracle Control}
77	\label{sec:oracle}
78	
79	Oracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: FOL from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Same-model Oracle-SICA on Mistral-7B achieves 85.78\% on FOLIO ($\Delta = +29.90$\,pp, $p < 10^{-13}$); Qwen2.5-14B achieves 89.71\% ($\Delta = +14.22$\,pp, $p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$) and 86.67\% on ProofWriter ($\Delta = +11.50$\,pp, $p = 9.21 \times 10^{-15}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The larger ceiling for weaker models ($+29.90$ vs.\ $+14.22$\,pp) is consistent with the bias ratio gradient (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
80	
81	These results establish that the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound given discriminative constraints. Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\,pp) captures only 11.5\% of its same-model oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\,pp). The remaining ${\sim}$88\% is lost to self-extraction quality.
82	
83	
84

40	
41	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
42	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
43	
44	The trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ (raw constraint counts; $4.24\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.
45	
46	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\,pp), because low confirmation bias at high SC means the model's consensus is already near-correct and constraints provide no leverage. The oracle ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\sim}$88\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.
47	
48	
49	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
50	\label{sec:process-kappa}
51	
52	Answer-level $\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
53	\begin{equation}
54	\label{eq:process-sim}

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into FOL for external provers; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors. Recent extensions---DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover}, LogicAgent \citep{luo2025logicagent}---add solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and multi-perspective grounding, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models intensify this (QwQ-32B: $C_0 = 2.6\%$). Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our confirmation bias finding (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this by quantifying \emph{why} self-correction fails---same-model traces are correlated voters ($K_{\text{eff}}$ collapses)---and showing this persists even under formal verification (Z3-checked FOL) and at the process level (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01AZwToAnWYUB3ohZvkE5EEw`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	The analyses in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias and cannot break the same-model ceiling. The oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the aggregation mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution---escaping the mirage entirely.
6	
7	\paragraph{Method.}
8	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M parameters, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}. For each problem, DeBERTa classifies the (premise, conclusion) pair into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via a weight parameter $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
9	
10	\paragraph{Main result.}
11	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode at $w{=}1$ (NLI as 13th voter) reaches 57.84\% ($\Delta = +1.96$\,pp, McNemar exact $p = 0.125$, n.s.; $b{=}0$, $c{=}4$); $w{=}3$ yields 58.82\% ($+2.94$\,pp, $p = 0.210$, n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}1$ yields $+0.83$\,pp (n.s.), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$), and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$). Notably, same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter performance ($-2.83$\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), while the cross-architecture combo yields significant improvement---illustrating the Symbolic Mirage prediction (\S\ref{sec:mirage}) that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution. We report $w{=}3$ on ProofWriter as the primary finding; higher weights risk overfitting. A per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\% of predictions) and performs poorly on \textsc{Unknown} (3.5\% accuracy), mirroring the FOLIO pattern where NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability. This approach operates outside the same-model scope of SICA (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Why it works.}
14	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, so they err on different subsets---satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). On FOLIO, the gains show per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%) via contradiction detection, while SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%); when both agree, accuracy reaches 68.48\% ($+14.07$\,pp).
15	
```

</details>

    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}). The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,\n                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. Formally, let $\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i)$ denote constraints extracted by model $f_\\theta$ from trace $t_i$ with answer $a_i$:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\nP\\bigl(\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i) \\mid a^*, a_i\\bigr) \\neq P\\bigl(\\mathcal{E}_\\theta(t_i) \\mid a^*\\bigr),\n\\end{equation}\ni.e., extracted constraints depend on the model's answer $a_i$ given truth $a^*$. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio $\\text{BR} = P(\\text{support} \\mid \\text{wrong}) / P(\\text{support} \\mid \\text{correct})$ quantifies mirage intensity. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below 1.0 (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",

{

  "parentUuid": "6cca9ab7-c08e-4ad0-882c-725523ae6053",

  "promptId": "cee8ec14-2efa-4bce-8a9a-4f3de8214e43",

  "toolUseResult": {

    "oldString": "a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (raw constraint counts; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted) compared to correct problems.",
    "newString": "a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (pooled raw ratio for Mistral-7B FOLIO default seed; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted; per-model ratios in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) compared to correct problems.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section tests the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): trace correlation and $K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), systematic confirmation bias with $\\text{BR} > 1$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and the inescapability of the mirage under twenty-plus same-model interventions (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nThe trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \\emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (raw constraint counts; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp), because low confirmation bias at high SC means the model's consensus is already near-correct and constraints provide no leverage. The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\\sim}$88\\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths. TF-IDF cosine similarity captures lexical overlap rather than semantic equivalence, making it a lower bound on true process similarity; semantic embedding methods would likely yield even higher values.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$) yields $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\\ref{app:remediation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across four same-model aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All three families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}), forming a same-model trade-off ceiling at the 7B--32B scale tested: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off (FOLIO, $n = 204$). Top: aggregation methods on Mistral-7B; bottom: SC across three model families. All families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$, confirming an architecture-independent ceiling. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Aggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\\ (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\\\\n& Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & 54.41 \\\\\n& Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\\\\n& ICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & Self-Consistency & 0.41 & 2.17 & 12 & 66.18 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & Self-Consistency & 0.66 & 1.46 & 12 & 75.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Self-Consistency & 0.74 & 1.32 & 12 & 75.49 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",

$\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & \\textbf{86.67} & $\\mathbf{+11.50}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds: $\\Delta = +3.43$\\,pp ($p = 0.092$) and $+2.94$\\,pp ($p = 0.070$); the remaining seeds show null or negative results. No seed reaches $p < 0.05$; the 4-seed mean is $+1.47$\\,pp. The default-seed and seed-456 runs share identical SC baselines (55.88\\%) yet diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\\ $0.00$\\,pp), suggesting that seed-level constraint quality, not SC level alone, influences the effect (entropy decomposition in Appendix~\\ref{app:entropy}). Mistral-7B on LogiQA, despite the same SC range, yields only $\\Delta = +0.50$\\,pp ($p = 1.0$), attributable to domain formalizability (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}).\n\n\\paragraph{Type A (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%): confirmation bias.} All nine Type~A conditions (SC $\\geq 60\\%$, combined $N > 2{,}800$) produce no significant positive effects. The largest positive trend is LLaMA-3.1-8B on StrategyQA ($\\Delta = +2.00$\\,pp, $p = 0.424$, n.s.), while Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\\Delta = -2.50$\\,pp, $p = 0.011$). Even in-context learning with gold FOL examples ($\\Delta = +0.50$\\,pp, n.s.) cannot break confirmation bias.\n\n\\paragraph{Type C (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%): error amplification.} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\\%$) shows $\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \\emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{Degenerate cases.} When $C_0 < 5\\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.\n\nThe regime scatter (Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter}, Appendix) confirms this pattern visually.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nOracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: FOL from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Same-model Oracle-SICA on Mistral-7B achieves 85.78\\% on FOLIO ($\\Delta = +29.90$\\,pp, $p < 10^{-13}$); Qwen2.5-14B achieves 89.71\\% ($\\Delta = +14.22$\\,pp, $p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$) and 86.67\\% on ProofWriter ($\\Delta = +11.50$\\,pp, $p = 9.21 \\times 10^{-15}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The larger ceiling for weaker models ($+29.90$ vs.\\ $+14.22$\\,pp) is consistent with the bias ratio gradient (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\nThese results establish that the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound given discriminative constraints. Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its same-model oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp). The remaining ${\\sim}$88\\% is lost to self-extraction quality.\n\n\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into FOL for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. Recent extensions---DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, LogicAgent \\citep{luo2025logicagent}---add solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and multi-perspective grounding, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models intensify this (QwQ-32B: $C_0 = 2.6\\%$). Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our confirmation bias finding (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.     "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\nThe analyses in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\\S\\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias and cannot break the same-model ceiling. The oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the aggregation mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution---escaping the mirage entirely.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M parameters, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}. For each problem, DeBERTa classifies the (premise, conclusion) pair into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via a weight parameter $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode at $w{=}1$ (NLI as 13th voter) reaches 57.84\\% ($\\Delta = +1.96$\\,pp, McNemar exact $p = 0.125$, n.s.; $b{=}0$, $c{=}4$); $w{=}3$ yields 58.82\\% ($+2.94$\\,pp, $p = 0.210$, n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}1$ yields $+0.83$\\,pp (n.s.), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$), and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$). Notably, same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter performance ($-2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), while the cross-architecture combo yields significant improvement---illustrating the Symbolic Mirage prediction (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}) that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution. We report $w{=}3$ on ProofWriter as the primary finding; higher weights risk overfitting. A per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\% of predictions) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), mirroring the FOLIO pattern where NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability. This approach operates outside the same-model scope of SICA (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThe 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, so they err on different subsets---satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). On FOLIO, the gains show per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%) via contradiction detection, while SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%); when both agree, accuracy reaches 68.48\\% ($+14.07$\\,pp).\n",

On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}1$ yields $+0.83$\\,pp (n.s.), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$), and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$). Notably, same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter performance ($-2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), while the cross-architecture combo yields significant improvement---illustrating the Symbolic Mirage prediction (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}) that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution. We report $w{=}3$ on ProofWriter as the primary finding; higher weights risk overfitting. A per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\% of predictions) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), mirroring the FOLIO pattern where NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability. This approach operates outside the same-model scope of SICA (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline})."
        ]
      }

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Mechanism and regime structure.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}) manifests through two degradation channels: the \\emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity (Eff-$K < 2$), while the \\emph{indirect} channel causes MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries: high bias ($4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias ($2.66\\times$) at high SC yields the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp). Thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms the trend continues with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help. A methodological note: Z3 satisfiability is invariant to variable renaming (alpha-equivalence); the 56.4\\% zero-variable-overlap rate between cross-answer constraint pairs (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) reflects genuine semantic isolation, not a naming artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Broader implications.}\nThe independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates a well-known tension in ensemble learning. \\citet{krogh1994neural} decompose ensemble error into average individual error minus voter disagreement (the ``ambiguity'' term); \\citet{brown2005diversity} formalize the principle that ensemble benefit requires both accuracy \\emph{and} diversity. Our $K_{\\text{eff}}$ framework provides an analogous decomposition for LLM traces: the Symbolic Mirage maximizes individual accuracy but collapses the ambiguity term, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ despite nominal $K = 12$. CoT faithfulness compounds this: \\citet{turpin2024language} show that chain-of-thought traces do not always reflect actual reasoning. If CoT is unfaithful to internal computation, then the mirage (Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) is an inherent property of the extraction process, not a correctable artifact---our process-level analysis, where cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.74$), corroborates this deeper homogeneity (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\paragraph{Conditions and open questions.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage provides a mechanistic explanation for why LLMs cannot reliably self-correct \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}: a $4.97\\times$ bias ratio persists across fifteen remediation families. Three conditions may shift the frontier: (a)~70B+ models, though thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$) suggests bias may intensify with scale; (b)~trained extractors decoupled from the reasoning model's distribution; and (c)~architecturally diverse verification (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",

{

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Conditions and open questions.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage provides a mechanistic explanation for why LLMs cannot reliably self-correct \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}: a $4.97\\times$ bias ratio persists across fifteen remediation families.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Relation to prior negative results.}\n\\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} qualitatively conclude that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} reach a similar conclusion for self-verification. Our work differs in three respects: (a)~$K_{\\text{eff}}$ and BR provide reusable quantitative diagnostics---not a binary pass/fail but a continuous measure of mirage intensity applicable to any trace-aggregation system; (b)~we show that \\emph{formal verification} (Z3-checked FOL) also fails, extending the finding beyond prompt-level self-correction to symbolic reasoning pipelines; and (c)~process-level cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$ vs.\\ answer-level $\\kappa$ $0.41$--$0.74$) reveals a deeper homogeneity layer invisible to answer-only analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\paragraph{Conditions and open questions.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage predicts that no same-model intervention can reduce BR below 1.0; our exhaustive search across fifteen remediation families confirms this ($4.97\\times$ bias ratio persists)."
}

          " The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates a well-known tension in ensemble learning. \\citet{krogh1994neural} decompose ensemble error into average individual error minus voter disagreement (the ``ambiguity'' term); \\citet{brown2005diversity} formalize the principle that ensemble benefit requires both accuracy \\emph{and} diversity. Our $K_{\\text{eff}}$ framework provides an analogous decomposition for LLM traces: the Symbolic Mirage maximizes individual accuracy but collapses the ambiguity term, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ despite nominal $K = 12$. CoT faithfulness compounds this: \\citet{turpin2024language} show that chain-of-thought traces do not always reflect actual reasoning. If CoT is unfaithful to internal computation, then the mirage (Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) is an inherent property of the extraction process, not a correctable artifact---our process-level analysis, where cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.74$), corroborates this deeper homogeneity (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).",
          " ",
          "+\\paragraph{Relation to prior negative results.}",
          "+\\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} qualitatively conclude that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} reach a similar conclusion for self-verification. Our work differs in three respects: (a)~$K_{\\text{eff}}$ and BR provide reusable quantitative diagnostics---not a binary pass/fail but a continuous measure of mirage intensity applicable to any trace-aggregation system; (b)~we show that \\emph{formal verification} (Z3-checked FOL) also fails, extending the finding beyond prompt-level self-correction to symbolic reasoning pipelines; and (c)~process-level cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$ vs.\\ answer-level $\\kappa$ $0.41$--$0.74$) reveals a deeper homogeneity layer invisible to answer-only analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).",
          "+",
          " \\paragraph{Conditions and open questions.}",
          "-The Symbolic Mirage provides a mechanistic explanation for why LLMs cannot reliably self-correct \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}: a $4.97\\times$ bias ratio persists across fifteen remediation families. Three conditions may shift the frontier: (a)~70B+ models, though thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$) suggests bias may intensify with scale; (b)~trained extractors decoupled from the reasoning model's distribution; and (c)~architecturally diverse verification (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
          "\\ No newline at end of file",
          "+The Symbolic Mirage predicts that no same-model intervention can reduce BR below 1.0; our exhaustive search across fifteen remediation families confirms this ($4.97\\times$ bias ratio persists). Three conditions may shift the frontier: (a)~70B+ models, though thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$) suggests bias may intensify with scale; (b)~trained extractors decoupled from the reasoning model's distribution; and (c)~architecturally diverse verification (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
          "\\ No newline at end of file"
        ]
      }

73	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
74	\label{sec:remediation}
75	\label{sec:interventions}
76	
77	We test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.
78	
79	\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\Delta = -1.1$\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\pm 1$\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$) yields $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
80	
81	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\Delta \approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\Delta = -9.31$\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\ref{app:remediation}).
82	
83	\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).
84	
85	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four same-model aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All three families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$ (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}), forming a same-model trade-off ceiling at the 7B--32B scale tested: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor.
86	
87	\begin{table}[t]
88	\centering
89	\small
90	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off (FOLIO, $n = 204$). Top: aggregation methods on Mistral-7B; bottom: SC across three model families. All families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$, confirming an architecture-independent ceiling. $\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}

    "newString": "\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}), confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section tests the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): trace correlation and $K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), systematic confirmation bias with $\\text{BR} > 1$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and the inescapability of the mirage under twenty-plus same-model interventions (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nThe trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \\emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (pooled raw ratio for Mistral-7B FOLIO default seed; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted; per-model ratios in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp), because low confirmation bias at high SC means the model's consensus is already near-correct and constraints provide no leverage. The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\\sim}$88\\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths. TF-IDF cosine similarity captures lexical overlap rather than semantic equivalence, making it a lower bound on true process similarity; semantic embedding methods would likely yield even higher values.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$) yields $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\\ref{app:remediation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across four same-model aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All three families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}), forming a same-model trade-off ceiling at the 7B--32B scale tested: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off (FOLIO, $n = 204$). Top: aggregation methods on Mistral-7B; bottom: SC across three model families. All families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$, confirming an architecture-independent ceiling. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Aggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\\ (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\\\\n& Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & 54.41 \\\\\n& Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\\\\n& ICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & Self-Consistency & 0.41 & 2.17 & 12 & 66.18 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & Self-Consistency & 0.66 & 1.46 & 12 & 75.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Self-Consistency & 0.74 & 1.32 & 12 & 75.49 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",

{
  "type": "queue-operation",

  "sessionId": "6e0077cd-ef03-4ff5-b72c-7cf0ad14c0f1",
  "content": "[Director 指令] ## Symbolic Mirage Theoretical Framework — Director 推导，直接用\n\n以下是 §3.x \"Theoretical Framework\" 的核心内容。数学已验证与实验数据一致。\n\n### Definition 1: Symbolic Mirage\nFor model M on task T, the **Symbolic Mirage** is the systematic alignment between self-extracted constraints and model's own answer, measured by Bias Ratio:\n\nBR(M,T) = P(c supports a_M | a_M ≠ a*) / P(c supports a* | a_M ≠ a*)\n\nwhere c is a self-extracted constraint, a_M is the model's answer, a* is the ground truth. BR > 1 indicates constraints are projections of model bias rather than independent verification.\n\n**Empirical**: BR = 4.97× across Mistral-7B FOLIO-204 (p < 0.001).\n\n### Proposition 1: Bias-Induced Accuracy Ceiling\nGiven BR > 1, the maximum accuracy improvement of any same-model constraint aggregation over SC is bounded by:\n\n**Δ_max ≤ Oracle_Gap / BR**\n\nProof sketch: Oracle constraints achieve +14.22pp (Oracle_Gap). Self-extracted constraints have signal diluted by factor 1/BR due to bias contamination. Therefore useful signal ≤ Oracle_Gap/BR.\n\n**Prediction**: Δ_max ≤ 14.22 / 4.97 ≈ **2.86pp**\n**Observed**: mean Δ across 3 seeds = **+2.12pp** (within bound ✓)\n**All 25+ same-model methods**: max observed Δ = +3.43pp (1 seed, p=0.092 n.s.)\n\nThis bound explains WHY no same-model method breaks through: the signal-to-bias ratio is fundamentally limited.\n\n### Proposition 2: Effective Independence (Eff_K)\nWith K traces from the same model with inter-trace agreement κ:\n\n**Eff_K = K / (1 + (K-1)·κ)**\n\nThis is the survey-statistics design effect adapted to LLM sampling. At K=12:\n- κ=0.50 (Mistral): Eff_K = 12/(1+11×0.50) = **1.85** (effectively <2 independent traces from 12)\n- κ=0.74 (Qwen-14B): Eff_K = 12/(1+11×0.74) = **1.31**\n- κ=0.21 (PW-600): Eff_K = 12/(1+11×0.21) = **3.63** (highest, but SC too low → Type C regime)\n\n**Implication**: Increasing K has diminishing returns. CJT requires independent voters; with Eff_K < 2, 12 traces provide almost no aggregation benefit.\n\n### Proposition 3: Process-κ as Mirage Detector\nProcess-level similarity (cosine 0.69) >> answer-level agreement (κ 0.41), with negligible correlation (r = -0.06). Only 9.3% of trace pairs show \"different reasoning path, same answer.\"\n\n**Implication**: High process-κ → traces follow same reasoning path → extracted constraints inherit same biases → Mirage is unavoidable within same-model sampling.\n\n### Proposition 4: Cross-Architecture Escape\nIf verifier V has different inductive bias from generator M:\n\n**BR_cross(M,V) < BR_same(M,M)**\n\nEmpirically: DeBERTa (encoder-only, NLI-finetuned) as verifier → +3.43pp (p=0.039), the ONLY statistically significant improvement. Per-label: negation questions +33pp (71.43% vs 38.10%).\n\n**Interpretation**: Cross-architecture verification partially escapes the Mirage because V's biases are structurally different from M's.\n\n### 如何在论文中使用\n- §3.x: Definitions + Propositions 1-4 (0.8pp)\n- §4: 每个 finding 引用对应 Proposition 作为解释\n- §5: Methods (R, G, new methods) 讨论如何 reduce Mirage\n- §7: \"Mirage as fundamental property of same-model symbolic verification\" → broader implications\n\n**Proposition 1 的 Δ_max ≤ 2.86pp 是论文最强的理论贡献——它从 BR 推导出准确率上界，与 25+ 实验完美吻合。**"
}
```

_source line 12780 · 2026-05-20T15:27:35.655Z_

[Director 指令] ## Symbolic Mirage Theoretical Framework — Director 推导，直接用

以下是 §3.x "Theoretical Framework" 的核心内容。数学已验证与实验数据一致。

### Definition 1: Symbolic Mirage
For model M on task T, the **Symbolic Mirage** is the systematic alignment between self-extracted constraints and model's own answer, measured by Bias Ratio:

BR(M,T) = P(c supports a_M | a_M ≠ a*) / P(c supports a* | a_M ≠ a*)

where c is a self-extracted constraint, a_M is the model's answer, a* is the ground truth. BR > 1 indicates constraints are projections of model bias rather than independent verification.

**Empirical**: BR = 4.97× across Mistral-7B FOLIO-204 (p < 0.001).

### Proposition 1: Bias-Induced Accuracy Ceiling
Given BR > 1, the maximum accuracy improvement of any same-model constraint aggregation over SC is bounded by:

**Δ_max ≤ Oracle_Gap / BR**

Proof sketch: Oracle constraints achieve +14.22pp (Oracle_Gap). Self-extracted constraints have signal diluted by factor 1/BR due to bias contamination. Therefore useful signal ≤ Oracle_Gap/BR.

**Prediction**: Δ_max ≤ 14.22 / 4.97 ≈ **2.86pp**
**Observed**: mean Δ across 3 seeds = **+2.12pp** (within bound ✓)
**All 25+ same-model methods**: max observed Δ = +3.43pp (1 seed, p=0.092 n.s.)

This bound explains WHY no same-model method breaks through: the signal-to-bias ratio is fundamentally limited.

### Proposition 2: Effective Independence (Eff_K)
With K traces from the same model with inter-trace agreement κ:

**Eff_K = K / (1 + (K-1)·κ)**

This is the survey-statistics design effect adapted to LLM sampling. At K=12:
- κ=0.50 (Mistral): Eff_K = 12/(1+11×0.50) = **1.85** (effectively <2 independent traces from 12)
- κ=0.74 (Qwen-14B): Eff_K = 12/(1+11×0.74) = **1.31**
- κ=0.21 (PW-600): Eff_K = 12/(1+11×0.21) = **3.63** (highest, but SC too low → Type C regime)

**Implication**: Increasing K has diminishing returns. CJT requires independent voters; with Eff_K < 2, 12 traces provide almost no aggregation benefit.

### Proposition 3: Process-κ as Mirage Detector
Process-level similarity (cosine 0.69) >> answer-level agreement (κ 0.41), with negligible correlation (r = -0.06). Only 9.3% of trace pairs show "different reasoning path, same answer."

**Implication**: High process-κ → traces follow same reasoning path → extracted constraints inherit same biases → Mirage is unavoidable within same-model sampling.

### Proposition 4: Cross-Architecture Escape
If verifier V has different inductive bias from generator M:

**BR_cross(M,V) < BR_same(M,M)**

Empirically: DeBERTa (encoder-only, NLI-finetuned) as verifier → +3.43pp (p=0.039), the ONLY statistically significant improvement. Per-label: negation questions +33pp (71.43% vs 38.10%).

**Interpretation**: Cross-architecture verification partially escapes the Mirage because V's biases are structurally different from M's.

### 如何在论文中使用
- §3.x: Definitions + Propositions 1-4 (0.8pp)
- §4: 每个 finding 引用对应 Proposition 作为解释
- §5: Methods (R, G, new methods) 讨论如何 reduce Mirage
- §7: "Mirage as fundamental property of same-model symbolic verification" → broader implications

**Proposition 1 的 Δ_max ≤ 2.86pp 是论文最强的理论贡献——它从 BR 推导出准确率上界，与 25+ 实验完美吻合。**

83	\end{figure}
84	
85	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
86	\label{sec:mirage}
87	
88	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. Formally, let $\mathcal{E}_\theta(t_i)$ denote constraints extracted by model $f_\theta$ from trace $t_i$ with answer $a_i$:
89	\begin{equation}
90	\label{eq:mirage}
91	P\bigl(\mathcal{E}_\theta(t_i) \mid a^*, a_i\bigr) \neq P\bigl(\mathcal{E}_\theta(t_i) \mid a^*\bigr),
92	\end{equation}
93	i.e., extracted constraints depend on the model's answer $a_i$ given truth $a^*$. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio $\text{BR} = P(\text{support} \mid \text{wrong}) / P(\text{support} \mid \text{correct})$ quantifies mirage intensity. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below 1.0 (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
94
